"""Raw ingest DAO — table raw_ingest (CSV rows before/after cleaning)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Optional

from dao.base_dao import BaseDAO
from database.connection import db_cursor


class RawIngestDAO(BaseDAO):
    table = "raw_ingest"

    def create(
        self,
        *,
        source_file: str,
        title: str = "",
        raw_text: str = "",
        category: str = "",
        published_date: str = "",
        source_type: str = "",
        cleaning_status: str = "pending",
        reject_reason: str | None = None,
        content_id: int | None = None,
    ) -> int:
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO raw_ingest (
                    source_file, title, raw_text, category, published_date,
                    source_type, cleaning_status, reject_reason, content_id
                )
                VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)
                RETURNING raw_ingest_id
                """,
                (
                    source_file,
                    title or None,
                    raw_text or None,
                    category or None,
                    published_date or None,
                    source_type or None,
                    cleaning_status,
                    reject_reason,
                    content_id,
                ),
            )
            return int(cur.fetchone()["raw_ingest_id"])

    def create_many(self, rows: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[int]:
        """Bulk insert in batches (one connection per batch)."""
        from config.runtime import db_insert_batch
        from utils.progress import progress

        if not rows:
            return []
        batch_size = db_insert_batch()
        ids: list[int] = []
        total_batches = (len(rows) + batch_size - 1) // batch_size
        sql = """
            INSERT INTO raw_ingest (
                source_file, title, raw_text, category, published_date,
                source_type, cleaning_status, reject_reason, content_id
            )
            VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)
            RETURNING raw_ingest_id
            """
        for batch_no, start in enumerate(range(0, len(rows), batch_size), start=1):
            batch = rows[start : start + batch_size]
            progress(f"DB insert batch {batch_no}/{total_batches} ({len(batch)} row(s))...")
            with db_cursor() as cur:
                for row in batch:
                    cur.execute(
                        sql,
                        (
                            str(row.get("source_file") or row.get("source_type") or "csv"),
                            str(row.get("title") or "") or None,
                            str(row.get("raw_text") or row.get("text") or row.get("_raw_text") or "") or None,
                            str(row.get("category") or "") or None,
                            str(row.get("date") or row.get("published_date") or "") or None,
                            str(row.get("source_type") or "") or None,
                            str(row.get("cleaning_status") or "pending"),
                            row.get("reject_reason") or row.get("_reject_reason"),
                            row.get("content_id"),
                        ),
                    )
                    ids.append(int(cur.fetchone()["raw_ingest_id"]))
        return ids

    def read(self, raw_ingest_id: int) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        return self.get_by_id("raw_ingest_id", raw_ingest_id)

    def update_status(
        self,
        raw_ingest_id: int,
        cleaning_status: str,
        *,
        reject_reason: str | None = None,
        content_id: int | None = None,
    ) -> int:
        return int(
            self.execute(
                """
                UPDATE raw_ingest
                SET cleaning_status = %s,
                    reject_reason = COALESCE(%s, reject_reason),
                    content_id = COALESCE(%s, content_id)
                WHERE raw_ingest_id = %s
                """,
                (cleaning_status, reject_reason, content_id, raw_ingest_id),
            )
        )

    def list_recent(self, limit: int = 20) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        rows = self.execute(
            """
            SELECT raw_ingest_id, source_file, title, category, cleaning_status,
                   reject_reason, content_id, ingested_at
            FROM raw_ingest
            ORDER BY raw_ingest_id DESC
            LIMIT %s
            """,
            (limit,),
            fetch="all",
        )
        return [dict(r) for r in rows or []]

    def count_by_status(self) -> dict[str, int]:
        rows = self.execute(
            """
            SELECT cleaning_status, COUNT(*) AS cnt
            FROM raw_ingest
            GROUP BY cleaning_status
            """,
            fetch="all",
        )
        return {str(r["cleaning_status"]): int(r["cnt"]) for r in rows or []}


==============================================================================
FILE: dao/README.md
==============================================================================
# Learning guide: `dao/`

## Why this folder exists

In a layered application, **Data Access Objects** are the only code that should know the exact shape of SQL for each table. Services reason about “create a user with this role”; DAOs reason about `INSERT INTO users (...) VALUES (%s, ...)` and which columns exist on the ERD.

NewsMind maps roughly one DAO file per major table (or tight table cluster). Keeping SQL here means:

- You can review database access in one directory.
- Repositories can compose multiple DAOs without duplicating JOIN patterns.
- Tests like `test_dao_crud.py` can hit the database boundary in isolation.

---

## How this folder connects upward and downward

**Below:** every DAO ultimately calls `database.connection.db_cursor` (or `BaseDAO.execute`, which wraps it). Rows come back as dicts thanks to `RealDictCursor`.

**Above:** `repository/` classes hold DAO instances and express **use-case sized** operations — for example `UserRepository.create_user` plus `ensure_desk`, or `ContentRepository.save_content_bundle` that writes `content` and `news` in coordination.

**Sideways:** `entities/` dataclasses are the typed input to many `create()` methods (`User`, `Content`, `Employee`). DAOs validate IDs and strings through `utils.validators` before binding parameters.

Typical login path:

```
auth_service.authenticate
  → UserDAO.read_by_username  (JOIN employee + role)
  → UserDAO.record_login_success
```

Typical pipeline persist path:

```
PersistenceService.persist_raw_records
  → ContentRepository.raw  →  RawIngestDAO.create_many
```

---

## Technology concepts

### Parameterized SQL (`%s` placeholders)

DAOs use psycopg2-style `%s` placeholders, never f-string interpolation of user input. Dynamic **column lists** in `UserDAO.update` still only allow whitelisted field names (`role_id`, `password_hash`, `account_status`) before building the `SET` clause — that is defense in depth against accidental SQL injection from caller kwargs.

### `BaseDAO` — shared execution shell

`base_dao.py` defines:

- `execute(sql, params, fetch="none"|"one"|"all")` — runs inside `db_cursor`, returns rowcount or fetched rows.
- `get_by_id`, `delete_by_id`, `list_all` — simple table templates using `self.table`.

Specialized DAOs set `table = "users"` (etc.) and add JOIN reads, upserts, and domain-specific updates.

### Entities vs dict rows

- **Into the DAO:** `User` dataclass on `create`.
- **Out of the DAO:** `dict[str, Any]` rows for flexibility in services and console output. `read_by_id` often returns `dict(row)` from `fetchone()`.

### Upsert pattern in `UserDAO.create`

The insert uses `ON CONFLICT (username) DO UPDATE` so re-seeding or admin re-run can refresh password hash and role without manual deletes. Study that when learning idempotent seeds.

---

## File-by-file inventory

| File | Table / focus | Typical callers |
|------|----------------|-----------------|
| `base_dao.py` | Shared execute helpers | All DAOs |
| `user_dao.py` | `users` + JOIN employee, role | Auth, UserRepository |
| `employee_dao.py` | `employee` | UserRepository |
| `role_dao.py` | `role` | UserRepository, RBAC seed |
| `journalist_dao.py`, `editor_dao.py`, … | Desk profile tables | UserRepository.ensure_desk |
| `content_dao.py` | `content` | ContentRepository, PersistenceService |
| `article_dao.py`, `news_dao.py` | Subtype tables | ContentRepository, ContentService |
| `story_pitch_dao.py` | Pitches | ContentRepository |
| `category_dao.py` | Categories | ContentService |
| `evidence_dao.py` | Evidence links | ContentRepository |
| `raw_ingest_dao.py` | Staging CSV rows | PersistenceService, ContentService lists |
| `ingest_file_dao.py` | File registry + hashes | IngestionService |
| `cleaning_report_dao.py` | DB quality reports | ReportService, admin reports menu |

Each desk DAO usually exposes `read_by_user_id` and `ensure_for_user` so `UserRepository.ensure_desk` can attach the correct profile row when admin adds a user.

---

## Deep dive: `UserDAO` (authentication-critical)

**`create(user: User)`** — validates employee_id, role_id, username, password_hash, account_status; inserts with upsert on username.

**`read_by_id` / `read_by_username`** — SELECT with JOINs so auth and menus get email, names, `role_code`, `role_name` in one round trip.

**`require(user_id)`** — read or raise `NotFoundError` (consistent “missing row” semantics for services).

**`update(**fields)`** — whitelisted columns only; stamps `updated_at`.

**`update_password_hash`** — sets hash and `password_changed_at` (important for auditing password policy).

**`record_login_success`** — updates `last_login`.

Password changes never store plaintext — callers pass already-hashed strings from `hash_password`.

---

## Deep dive: pipeline-related DAOs

**`IngestFileDAO`** tracks which CSV paths were ingested and content hashes so `IngestionService.list_new_or_changed_files` can mark `[new/changed]` in the admin menu.

**`RawIngestDAO`** stores pre-clean and rejected rows with `cleaning_status` and optional `reject_reason`, linking forward to `content_id` when persisted.

**`CleaningReportDAO`** stores JSON summaries from cleaning runs for **List cleaning reports** / **View report** in `AdminModule`.

---

## Design rules you should follow when adding a DAO

1. Import `db_cursor` or extend `BaseDAO`; do not open raw connections elsewhere.
2. Validate with `utils.validators` at the DAO boundary when the ERD column has business rules.
3. Return dicts for reads; accept entities or explicit primitives for writes.
4. Keep JOINs in the DAO that owns the primary table (e.g. user-centric JOINs stay in `UserDAO`).

---

## Exercises

1. Pick `content_dao.py` and trace `create` from `Content` entity to `RETURNING content_id`.
2. Explain why `read_by_username` JOINs `employee` — what would break in `auth_service._user_payload` without email?
3. Run `tests/test_dao_crud.py` with Postgres up and map each test to a DAO method.
4. Compare `BaseDAO.list_all` with a specialized `list_recent` in `ContentDAO` — when is generic listing not enough?


==============================================================================
FILE: dao/role_dao.py
==============================================================================
"""Role DAO — table role."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Optional

from dao.base_dao import BaseDAO
from database.connection import db_cursor
from entities.role import Role
from utils.errors import NotFoundError, ValidationError
from utils.validators import require_nonempty, require_optional_str, require_positive_id, require_role_code


class RoleDAO(BaseDAO):
    table = "role"

    def create(self, role: Role) -> int:
        code = require_role_code(role.role_code)
        name = require_nonempty(role.role_name, "Role name")
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO role (role_code, role_name, role_description, is_active)
                VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s)
                ON CONFLICT (role_code) DO UPDATE
                SET role_name = EXCLUDED.role_name,
                    role_description = EXCLUDED.role_description,
                    is_active = EXCLUDED.is_active
                RETURNING role_id
                """,
                (
                    code,
                    name,
                    require_optional_str(role.role_description, "Role description"),
                    bool(role.is_active),
                ),
            )
            return int(cur.fetchone()["role_id"])

    def get_id_by_code(self, role_code: str) -> Optional[int]:
        code = require_role_code(role_code)
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute("SELECT role_id FROM role WHERE role_code = %s", (code,))
            row = cur.fetchone()
            return int(row["role_id"]) if row else None

    def read(self, role_id: int) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        return self.get_by_id("role_id", require_positive_id(role_id, "Role ID"))

    def require(self, role_id: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
        row = self.read(role_id)
        if not row:
            raise NotFoundError(f"Role ID {role_id} was not found.")
        return row

    def update(self, role_id: int, **fields: Any) -> int:
        self.require(role_id)
        allowed = {"role_name", "role_description", "is_active"}
        cols = {k: v for k, v in fields.items() if k in allowed}
        if "role_name" in cols:
            cols["role_name"] = require_nonempty(cols["role_name"], "Role name")
        if not cols:
            raise ValidationError("No valid fields to update.")
        sets = ", ".join(f"{k} = %s" for k in cols)
        return int(
            self.execute(
                f"UPDATE role SET {sets}, updated_at = NOW() WHERE role_id = %s",
                (*cols.values(), role_id),
            )
        )

    def delete(self, role_id: int) -> int:
        self.require(role_id)
        return self.delete_by_id("role_id", role_id)


==============================================================================
FILE: dao/story_pitch_dao.py
==============================================================================
"""StoryPitch DAO — table story_pitch."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Optional

from dao.base_dao import BaseDAO
from database.connection import db_cursor
from entities.content.story_pitch import StoryPitch
from utils.errors import NotFoundError, ValidationError
from utils.validators import (
    require_nonempty,
    require_optional_positive_id,
    require_optional_str,
    require_pitch_status,
    require_positive_id,
    require_priority,
    require_score,
    require_title,
)


class StoryPitchDAO(BaseDAO):
    table = "story_pitch"

    def create(self, pitch: StoryPitch) -> int:
        title = require_title(pitch.title)
        summary = require_nonempty(pitch.summary, "Summary")
        status = require_pitch_status(pitch.status or "submitted")
        priority = require_priority(pitch.priority or "medium")
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO story_pitch (
                    audience_insight_id, title, summary, priority, source_type,
                    trend_score, audience_interest_score, coverage_gap_score,
                    status, created_by, approved_by, submitted_at, approved_at
                )
                VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s, %s)
                RETURNING story_pitch_id
                """,
                (
                    require_optional_positive_id(pitch.audience_insight_id, "Audience insight ID"),
                    title,
                    summary,
                    priority,
                    require_optional_str(pitch.source_type, "Source type", max_len=64),
                    require_score(pitch.trend_score, "Trend score"),
                    require_score(pitch.audience_interest_score, "Audience interest score"),
                    require_score(pitch.coverage_gap_score, "Coverage gap score"),
                    status,
                    require_optional_positive_id(pitch.created_by, "Created by"),
                    require_optional_positive_id(pitch.approved_by, "Approved by"),
                    pitch.submitted_at,
                    pitch.approved_at,
                ),
            )
            return int(cur.fetchone()["story_pitch_id"])

    def read(self, story_pitch_id: int) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        return self.get_by_id("story_pitch_id", require_positive_id(story_pitch_id, "Story pitch ID"))

    def require(self, story_pitch_id: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
        row = self.read(story_pitch_id)
        if not row:
            raise NotFoundError(f"Story pitch ID {story_pitch_id} was not found.")
        return row

    def update(self, story_pitch_id: int, **fields: Any) -> int:
        self.require(story_pitch_id)
        allowed = {
            "title",
            "summary",
            "priority",
            "source_type",
            "trend_score",
            "audience_interest_score",
            "coverage_gap_score",
            "status",
            "approved_by",
            "approved_at",
        }
        cols = {k: v for k, v in fields.items() if k in allowed}
        if "title" in cols:
            cols["title"] = require_title(cols["title"])
        if "summary" in cols:
            cols["summary"] = require_nonempty(cols["summary"], "Summary")
        if "status" in cols:
            cols["status"] = require_pitch_status(str(cols["status"]))
        if "priority" in cols:
            cols["priority"] = require_priority(cols["priority"])
        if not cols:
            raise ValidationError("No valid fields to update.")
        sets = ", ".join(f"{k} = %s" for k in cols)
        return int(
            self.execute(
                f"UPDATE story_pitch SET {sets}, updated_at = NOW() WHERE story_pitch_id = %s",
                (*cols.values(), story_pitch_id),
            )
        )

    def update_status(self, story_pitch_id: int, status: str) -> int:
        return self.update(story_pitch_id, status=require_pitch_status(status))

    def list_by_status(self, status: str, limit: int = 100) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        """List pitches for a status, urgent/high first (Editor queue order)."""
        status = require_pitch_status(status)
        rows = self.execute(
            """
            SELECT * FROM story_pitch
            WHERE status = %s
            ORDER BY
                CASE lower(coalesce(priority, 'medium'))
                    WHEN 'urgent' THEN 0
                    WHEN 'high' THEN 1
                    WHEN 'medium' THEN 2
                    WHEN 'low' THEN 3
                    ELSE 4
                END,
                story_pitch_id DESC
            LIMIT %s
            """,
            (status, max(1, min(int(limit), 500))),
            fetch="all",
        )
        return [dict(r) for r in rows or []]

    def delete(self, story_pitch_id: int) -> int:
        self.require(story_pitch_id)
        return self.delete_by_id("story_pitch_id", story_pitch_id)

FILE: dao/user_dao.py
==============================================================================
"""User DAO — ERD USER / physical table users."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Optional

from dao.base_dao import BaseDAO
from database.connection import db_cursor
from entities.user import User
from utils.errors import NotFoundError, ValidationError
from utils.validators import (
    require_account_status,
    require_nonempty,
    require_positive_id,
    require_username,
)


class UserDAO(BaseDAO):
    table = "users"

    def create(self, user: User) -> int:
        employee_id = require_positive_id(user.employee_id, "Employee ID")
        role_id = require_positive_id(user.role_id, "Role ID")
        username = require_username(user.username)
        password_hash = require_nonempty(user.password_hash, "Password hash")
        status = require_account_status(user.account_status or "active")
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                INSERT INTO users (
                    employee_id, role_id, username, password_hash, account_status
                )
                VALUES (%s, %s, %s, %s, %s)
                ON CONFLICT (username) DO UPDATE
                SET password_hash = EXCLUDED.password_hash,
                    account_status = EXCLUDED.account_status,
                    role_id = EXCLUDED.role_id,
                    updated_at = NOW()
                RETURNING user_id
                """,
                (employee_id, role_id, username, password_hash, status),
            )
            return int(cur.fetchone()["user_id"])

    def read(self, user_id: int) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        return self.read_by_id(user_id)

    def read_by_id(self, user_id: int) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        user_id = require_positive_id(user_id, "User ID")
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                SELECT u.*, e.email, e.first_name, e.last_name, r.role_code, r.role_name
                FROM users u
                JOIN employee e ON e.employee_id = u.employee_id
                JOIN role r ON r.role_id = u.role_id
                WHERE u.user_id = %s
                """,
                (user_id,),
            )
            row = cur.fetchone()
            return dict(row) if row else None

    def require(self, user_id: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
        row = self.read_by_id(user_id)
        if not row:
            raise NotFoundError(f"User ID {user_id} was not found.")
        return row

    def read_by_username(self, username: str) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
        username = require_username(username)
        with db_cursor() as cur:
            cur.execute(
                """
                SELECT u.*, e.email, e.first_name, e.last_name, r.role_code, r.role_name
                FROM users u
                JOIN employee e ON e.employee_id = u.employee_id
                JOIN role r ON r.role_id = u.role_id
                WHERE u.username = %s
                """,
                (username,),
            )
            row = cur.fetchone()
            return dict(row) if row else None

    def update(self, user_id: int, **fields: Any) -> int:
        self.require(user_id)
        allowed = {"role_id", "password_hash", "account_status"}
        cols = {k: v for k, v in fields.items() if k in allowed}
        if "role_id" in cols:
            cols["role_id"] = require_positive_id(cols["role_id"], "Role ID")
        if "password_hash" in cols:
            cols["password_hash"] = require_nonempty(cols["password_hash"], "Password hash")
        if "account_status" in cols:
            cols["account_status"] = require_account_status(cols["account_status"])
        if not cols:
            raise ValidationError("No valid fields to update.")
        sets = ", ".join(f"{k} = %s" for k in cols)
        return int(
            self.execute(
                f"UPDATE users SET {sets}, updated_at = NOW() WHERE user_id = %s",
                (*cols.values(), user_id),
            )
        )

    def update_account_status(self, user_id: int, status: str) -> int:
        return self.update(user_id, account_status=require_account_status(status))

    def update_password_hash(self, user_id: int, password_hash: str) -> int:
        """Set a new password hash and stamp password_changed_at."""
        user_id = require_positive_id(user_id, "User ID")
        password_hash = require_nonempty(password_hash, "Password hash")
        self.require(user_id)
        return int(
            self.execute(
                """
                UPDATE users
                SET password_hash = %s,
                    password_changed_at = NOW(),
                    updated_at = NOW()
                WHERE user_id = %s
                """,
                (password_hash, user_id),
            )
        )

    def record_login_success(self, user_id: int) -> None:
        self.execute(
            "UPDATE users SET last_login = NOW(), updated_at = NOW() WHERE user_id = %s",
            (require_positive_id(user_id, "User ID"),),
        )

    def delete(self, user_id: int) -> int:
        self.require(user_id)
        return self.delete_by_id("user_id", user_id)


==============================================================================
FILE: database/connection.py
==============================================================================
"""PostgreSQL connectivity for the NewsMind console backend."""

from __future__ import annotations

from contextlib import contextmanager
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Generator, Optional

import psycopg2
from psycopg2.extensions import connection as PgConnection
from psycopg2.extras import RealDictCursor

from config.db_config import DatabaseConfig, load_db_config
from utils.logger import get_logger

log = get_logger("db")
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]


def get_connection(cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> PgConnection:
    cfg = cfg or load_db_config()
    return psycopg2.connect(**cfg.dsn_kwargs())


@contextmanager
def db_cursor(cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> Generator:
    cfg = cfg or load_db_config()
    conn = get_connection(cfg)
    try:
        with conn:
            with conn.cursor(cursor_factory=RealDictCursor) as cur:
                yield cur
    finally:
        conn.close()


def apply_sql_file(path: Path, cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> None:
    sql = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    cfg = cfg or load_db_config()
    conn = get_connection(cfg)
    try:
        with conn:
            with conn.cursor() as cur:
                cur.execute(sql)
        log.info("Applied SQL file %s", path.name)
    finally:
        conn.close()


def init_schema(cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> None:
    cfg = cfg or load_db_config()
    apply_sql_file(ROOT / "database" / "schema.sql", cfg)
    apply_sql_file(ROOT / "database" / "seed_data.sql", cfg)
    apply_sql_file(ROOT / "database" / "schema_cae_publisher.sql", cfg)
    from database.seed_demo_users import seed_demo_users

    seed_demo_users()


def ensure_cae_publisher_schema(cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> None:
    """Apply CAE/publisher tables without wiping the main schema."""
    apply_sql_file(ROOT / "database" / "schema_cae_publisher.sql", cfg or load_db_config())


def check_connection(cfg: Optional[DatabaseConfig] = None) -> bool:
    try:
        with db_cursor(cfg) as cur:
            cur.execute("SELECT 1 AS ok")
            row = cur.fetchone()
            return bool(row and row.get("ok") == 1)
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
        log.error("Database connection failed: %s", exc)
        return False


==============================================================================
FILE: database/README.md
==============================================================================
# Learning guide: `database/`

## Why this folder exists

Python business logic in NewsMind talks to **PostgreSQL**. Someone has to own connection strings, cursors, schema application, and seed data — that is this folder. DAOs should not each open their own ad-hoc connections with duplicated DSN logic; they import `db_cursor` from `connection.py` and focus on SQL for one table.

Think of `database/` as the **infrastructure layer** directly under `config/`: settings come from `.env` via `load_db_config()`, and everything that reads or writes a row flows through the helpers defined here.

---

## How this folder connects to the rest of the project

On first setup you run `python app.py --init-db`. That calls `init_schema()` in `connection.py`, which executes `schema.sql` and `seed_data.sql`, then runs `seed_demo_users.py` to ensure password hashes match the PBKDF2 format used at runtime.

Every DAO method that touches the database uses `with db_cursor() as cur:` (or `BaseDAO.execute`, which wraps the same pattern). The console’s **DB status** menu item calls `check_connection()`, which runs a trivial `SELECT 1` through the same cursor helper.

```
.env  →  config.db_config.load_db_config()
              ↓
database.connection.get_connection / db_cursor
              ↓
dao.*  +  apply_sql_file(schema)  +  seed_demo_users
```

`app.py` also imports `check_connection` and `init_schema` for `--status` and `--init-db` CLI flags.

---

## Technology concepts

### `psycopg2`

The PostgreSQL adapter for Python. `get_connection` calls `psycopg2.connect(**cfg.dsn_kwargs())` where `dsn_kwargs` comes from the frozen `DatabaseConfig` dataclass in `config/db_config.py`.

### `db_cursor` — the context manager pattern

```python
@contextmanager
def db_cursor(cfg=None):
    conn = get_connection(cfg)
    try:
        with conn:
            with conn.cursor(cursor_factory=RealDictCursor) as cur:
                yield cur
    finally:
        conn.close()
```

**Why a context manager?** It guarantees:

1. A connection is opened for the unit of work.
2. `with conn:` commits on success and rolls back on exception (psycopg2 transaction semantics).
3. The connection is **closed** in `finally`, even if something fails — avoiding connection leaks in a long-lived console session.

DAO code stays readable: one indented block per query batch instead of manual try/finally everywhere.

### `RealDictCursor`

A `psycopg2.extras` cursor factory. Each row is a **dict** keyed by column name (`row["user_id"]`) instead of a tuple (`row[0]`). That matches how the rest of the stack passes data — services and `app.py` print `dict` rows, and entities are built from named fields.

When you see `cur.fetchone()["user_id"]` in `UserDAO`, that only works because `db_cursor` sets `cursor_factory=RealDictCursor`.

### `apply_sql_file` vs ad-hoc DDL

`init_schema` reads `database/schema.sql` and `database/seed_data.sql` as text and executes them in one connection. That keeps the canonical ERD in SQL files you can diff in git, while Python only orchestrates apply order.

---

## File-by-file walkthrough

### `connection.py`

| Symbol | Role |
|--------|------|
| `ROOT` | Path to project root (`New folder`), used to locate SQL files. |
| `get_connection(cfg)` | Builds a live `psycopg2` connection from config. |
| `db_cursor(cfg)` | Yield a `RealDictCursor` inside a committed transaction; always closes connection. |
| `apply_sql_file(path, cfg)` | Run a `.sql` file’s contents once (used for schema + seed). |
| `init_schema(cfg)` | Apply schema, seed SQL, then Python demo user seeding. |
| `check_connection(cfg)` | `SELECT 1`; logs and returns `False` on any failure. |

**Logging:** module logger `db` records apply success and connection errors.

### `schema.sql`

Defines PostgreSQL tables for the newsroom ERD: users, employee, role, content, raw_ingest, ingest_file, cleaning_report, desk profile tables, and so on. This is the source of truth for column names DAOs must use.

### `seed_data.sql`

Reference rows: roles, categories, maybe structural data that does not depend on Python hashing.

### `seed_demo_users.py`

Creates or updates demo users with `hash_password` from `modules.authentication.security` so login passwords match `DEMO_PASSWORDS` in `config.constants`. Called at the end of `init_schema`.

---

## Who calls what

- **All DAOs** — `from database.connection import db_cursor` (directly or via `BaseDAO.execute`).
- **`app.py`** — `init_schema`, `check_connection` for setup and status.
- **`tests/conftest.py` and integration tests** — often call `init_schema()` when PostgreSQL is available.
- **No upward import** — `database/` does not import DAOs or services (clean dependency direction).

---

## Operational notes

- If `check_connection` fails, verify `.env` matches your local Postgres and that the database exists.
- Re-running `--init-db` reapplies schema/seed; understand whether your SQL uses `CREATE IF NOT EXISTS` or destructive drops before relying on it in production-like environments.
- Session auth is **not** stored in the database; only user credentials and newsroom data live here.

---

## Exercises

1. Trace one `UserDAO.read_by_username` call from SQL string to `db_cursor` exit — when does commit happen?
2. Temporarily break `NEWSROOM_DB_HOST` in `.env` and run **DB status** — read `application.log` for the `db` logger message.
3. Open `schema.sql`, find `users.password_hash`, and relate it to the `salt$digest` format in `security.py`.
4. Compare a query using `RealDictCursor` with what you would write using a default tuple cursor — why dict rows reduce bugs in this project?


==============================================================================
FILE: database/schema.sql
==============================================================================
-- NewsMind console — checklist + login/RBAC
-- ERD USER → physical table `users` (no app_user, no user_session)

BEGIN;

CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS "pgcrypto";

DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS public CASCADE;
CREATE SCHEMA public;
GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO PUBLIC;

CREATE TABLE employee (
    employee_id         BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    employee_code       VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    first_name          VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL,
    last_name           VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL,
    email               VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    mobile_number       VARCHAR(32),
    department          VARCHAR(128),
    designation         VARCHAR(128),
    location            VARCHAR(128),
    employment_status   VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    joining_date        DATE,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE role (
    role_id             BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    role_code           VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    role_name           VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL,
    role_description    TEXT,
    is_active           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- ERD entity USER (not app_user)
CREATE TABLE users (
    user_id             BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    employee_id         BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES employee(employee_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    role_id             BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES role(role_id) ON DELETE RESTRICT,
    username            VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    password_hash       TEXT NOT NULL,
    account_status      VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    last_login          TIMESTAMPTZ,
    password_changed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE audience_insight_analyst (
    aia_id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    focus_region        VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE editor (
    editor_id           BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    editorial_desk      VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE journalist (
    journalist_id       BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    primary_specialization   VARCHAR(128),
    secondary_specialization VARCHAR(128),
    availability_status VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'available',
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE content_adaptation_editor (
    cae_id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    specialization_channel VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE publisher (
    publisher_id        BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    publishing_group    VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE newsroom_manager (
    newsroom_manager_id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    user_id             BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    managed_region      VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE category (
    category_id         BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    category_name       VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    description         TEXT,
    is_active           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE story_pitch (
    story_pitch_id      BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    audience_insight_id BIGINT,
    title               VARCHAR(512) NOT NULL,
    summary             TEXT,
    priority            VARCHAR(32) DEFAULT 'medium',
    source_type         VARCHAR(64),
    trend_score         NUMERIC(8,4) DEFAULT 0,
    audience_interest_score NUMERIC(8,4) DEFAULT 0,
    coverage_gap_score  NUMERIC(8,4) DEFAULT 0,
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'submitted',
    created_by          BIGINT,
    approved_by         BIGINT,
    submitted_at        TIMESTAMPTZ,
    approved_at         TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Raw CSV ingest rows (before / after cleaning decision)
CREATE TABLE raw_ingest (
    raw_ingest_id       BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    source_file         TEXT NOT NULL,
    title               TEXT,
    raw_text            TEXT,
    category            VARCHAR(128),
    published_date      VARCHAR(64),
    source_type         VARCHAR(128),
    cleaning_status     VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
    reject_reason       TEXT,
    content_id          BIGINT,
    ingested_at         TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE content (
    content_id          BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    author_id           BIGINT REFERENCES journalist(journalist_id),
    title               VARCHAR(512) NOT NULL,
    summary             TEXT,
    raw_body            TEXT,
    content_body        TEXT,
    content_kind        VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'article',
    source_type         VARCHAR(128),
    status              VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'draft',
    current_version     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    submitted_at        TIMESTAMPTZ,
    approved_at         TIMESTAMPTZ,
    published_at        TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

ALTER TABLE raw_ingest
    ADD CONSTRAINT raw_ingest_content_fk
    FOREIGN KEY (content_id) REFERENCES content(content_id) ON DELETE SET NULL;

CREATE TABLE article (
    article_id          BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    content_id          BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES content(content_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    story_pitch_id      BIGINT REFERENCES story_pitch(story_pitch_id),
    article_type        VARCHAR(64) DEFAULT 'feature',
    estimated_read_time INTEGER
);

CREATE TABLE news (
    news_id             BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    content_id          BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES content(content_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    event_title         VARCHAR(512),
    event_type          VARCHAR(128),
    event_location      VARCHAR(255),
    event_date          TIMESTAMPTZ,
    breaking_news_flag  BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    severity            VARCHAR(32)
);

CREATE TABLE evidence (
    evidence_id         BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    content_id          BIGINT REFERENCES content(content_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    title               VARCHAR(512) NOT NULL,
    evidence_type       VARCHAR(64),
    source_url          TEXT,
    file_path           TEXT,
    ocr_text            TEXT,
    uploaded_by         BIGINT REFERENCES journalist(journalist_id),
    uploaded_at         TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Track which CSV files have been ingested (supports single-file / new-file runs)
CREATE TABLE ingest_file (
    ingest_file_id      BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    source_file         TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    file_hash           VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    row_count           INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    usable_row_count    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    last_status         VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ingested',
    last_ingested_at    TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Cleaning quality reports stored in DB (source of truth)
CREATE TABLE cleaning_report (
    cleaning_report_id  BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    report_type         VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL,
    source_files        TEXT,
    input_count         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    kept_count          INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    rejected_count      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    report_json         JSONB NOT NULL,
    created_by          BIGINT REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX idx_raw_ingest_status ON raw_ingest(cleaning_status);
CREATE INDEX idx_content_status ON content(status);
CREATE INDEX idx_ingest_file_hash ON ingest_file(file_hash);
CREATE INDEX idx_cleaning_report_created ON cleaning_report(created_at DESC);

COMMIT;

-- CAE adaptation + publisher tables (safe to re-run)
BEGIN;

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS adaptation_requests (
    id                  BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    original_title      VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL,
    original_content    TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_by          BIGINT REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    status              VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PROCESSING',
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS adaptation_drafts (
    id                  BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    request_id          BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES adaptation_requests(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    platform            VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL,
    version_number      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    adapted_title       VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL,
    adapted_content     TEXT NOT NULL,
    model_version       VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT '1.0.0',
    status              VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING_REVIEW',
    parent_draft_id     BIGINT REFERENCES adaptation_drafts(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    generation_parameters JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    validation_flags    JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_adaptation_drafts_request ON adaptation_drafts(request_id);
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_adaptation_drafts_platform ON adaptation_drafts(platform);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS adaptation_reviews (
    id                  BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    draft_id            BIGINT NOT NULL REFERENCES adaptation_drafts(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    action              VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL,
    remarks             TEXT,
    reviewed_by         BIGINT REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    reviewed_at         TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_adaptation_reviews_draft ON adaptation_reviews(draft_id);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS final_adaptations (
    id                  BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    draft_id            BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES adaptation_drafts(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    final_title         VARCHAR(500) NOT NULL,
    final_content       TEXT NOT NULL,
    finalized_by        BIGINT REFERENCES users(user_id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
    remarks             TEXT,
    finalized_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS publication (
    publication_id      BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    content_id          BIGINT NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES content(content_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    template_code       VARCHAR(64),
    status              VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'ready',
    scheduled_at        TIMESTAMPTZ,
    published_at        TIMESTAMPTZ,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_publication_status ON publication(status);

COMMIT;

==============================================================================
FILE: database/seed_demo_users.py
==============================================================================
"""Seed demo desks using usernames + passwords from docs/CREDENTIALS.md."""

from __future__ import annotations

from config.constants import DEMO_PASSWORDS, ROLE_DISPLAY_NAMES
from dao.audience_insight_analyst_dao import AudienceInsightAnalystDAO
from dao.content_adaptation_editor_dao import ContentAdaptationEditorDAO
from dao.editor_dao import EditorDAO
from dao.employee_dao import EmployeeDAO
from dao.journalist_dao import JournalistDAO
from dao.newsroom_manager_dao import NewsroomManagerDAO
from dao.publisher_dao import PublisherDAO
from dao.role_dao import RoleDAO
from dao.user_dao import UserDAO
from entities.employee import Employee
from entities.user import User
from modules.authentication.security import hash_password
from utils.logger import get_logger

log = get_logger("seed_users")

DEMO_USERS = (
    ("admin", "administrator", "System", "Admin", "admin@newsroom.local"),
    ("analyst", "audience_insight_analyst", "Asha", "Analyst", "analyst@newsroom.local"),
    ("journalist", "journalist", "Jordan", "Journalist", "journalist@newsroom.local"),
    ("editor", "editor", "Elena", "Editor", "editor@newsroom.local"),
    ("adapter", "content_adaptation_editor", "Casey", "Adapter", "adapter@newsroom.local"),
    ("publisher", "publisher", "Priya", "Publisher", "publisher@newsroom.local"),
    ("manager", "newsroom_manager", "Morgan", "Manager", "manager@newsroom.local"),
)


def _ensure_profile(user_id: int, role_code: str) -> None:
    if role_code == "audience_insight_analyst":
        AudienceInsightAnalystDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)
    elif role_code == "editor":
        EditorDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)
    elif role_code == "journalist":
        JournalistDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)
    elif role_code == "content_adaptation_editor":
        ContentAdaptationEditorDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)
    elif role_code == "publisher":
        PublisherDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)
    elif role_code == "newsroom_manager":
        NewsroomManagerDAO().ensure_for_user(user_id)


def seed_demo_users() -> int:
    employees = EmployeeDAO()
    users = UserDAO()
    roles = RoleDAO()
    created = 0
    for username, role_code, first, last, email in DEMO_USERS:
        role_id = roles.get_id_by_code(role_code)
        if role_id is None:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Missing role {role_code} — apply seed_data.sql first")
        emp_id = employees.create(
            Employee(
                employee_code=f"NF-{username.upper()}",
                first_name=first,
                last_name=last,
                email=email,
                department="Newsroom",
                designation=ROLE_DISPLAY_NAMES.get(role_code, role_code),
            )
        )
        user_id = users.create(
            User(
                employee_id=emp_id,
                role_id=role_id,
                username=username,
                password_hash=hash_password(DEMO_PASSWORDS[username]),
                account_status="active",
            )
        )
        _ensure_profile(user_id, role_code)
        created += 1
        log.info("Seeded user=%s role=%s", username, role_code)
    return created


==============================================================================
FILE: DEPLOYMENT.md
==============================================================================
# Deployment — console + hosted PostgreSQL

## Overview

The NewsMind console runs on the application host. PostgreSQL is configured through `.env` and may be a **hosted / remote** database.

---

## Runtime behaviour

1. **Chunked CSV reads** — large files are read in batches (`NEWSROOM_CSV_CHUNK_ROWS`).
2. **File-by-file pipeline** (default) — multi-file runs process each CSV: ingest → clean → persist, with console progress for each file.
3. **Batched raw inserts** — `raw_ingest` writes use `NEWSROOM_DB_INSERT_BATCH`.
4. **Processed snapshots** — optional local CSV/JSON under `data/processed`; quality reports are stored in `cleaning_report` (DB).
5. **Hosted DB options** — connect timeout, SSL mode, and TCP keepalives.

Progress for ingest / clean / persist steps is printed in the console and written to `logs/application.log`.

---

## `.env` example (hosted DB)

```env
NEWSROOM_DB_HOST=your-hosted-db.example.com
NEWSROOM_DB_PORT=5432
NEWSROOM_DB_NAME=newsmind_console
NEWSROOM_DB_USER=newsmind_app
NEWSROOM_DB_PASSWORD=***secret***
NEWSROOM_DB_SSLMODE=require
NEWSROOM_DB_CONNECT_TIMEOUT=15

NEWSROOM_CSV_CHUNK_ROWS=2000
NEWSROOM_DB_INSERT_BATCH=200
NEWSROOM_MAX_CSV_MB_WARN=15
NEWSROOM_WRITE_PROCESSED_FILES=false
NEWSROOM_PIPELINE_FILE_BY_FILE=true
NEWSROOM_DEBUG=false
```

---

## How to run

```powershell
cd "C:\path\to\NewsMindAI V2"
$env:PYTHONPATH = (Get-Location).Path
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python app.py --init-db
python app.py
```

### Recommended operator habits

| Prefer | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Pipeline one CSV file** | Clear progress per file |
| **Full pipeline (new files only)** | Skips unchanged hashes |
| Keep very large dumps outside `data/raw` until needed | Use `data/archive/` if you stage them |

---

## What is stored in the database

| Data | Table |
|---|---|
| Cleaned news | `content` |
| Raw / rejects | `raw_ingest` |
| File registry | `ingest_file` |
| Quality reports | `cleaning_report` |


==============================================================================
FILE: docs/NAMING.md
==============================================================================
# Naming guide (keep it simple)

This is a college project. Names should say **what** something is.

## Two different “priority” ideas (do not mix them)

| Name in code | Who uses it | Meaning |
|--------------|-------------|---------|
| **Editor queue urgency** | Editor / Manager | How soon should this story be reviewed? (`urgent` / `high` / `medium` / `low`) |
| **Pitch importance** | AIA story pitches | How important is this pitch idea? Simple formula. Stored in DB column `story_pitch.priority` |

- Model folder: `ML Project/editor_queue_urgency/`
- Model file: `artifacts/models/editor_queue_urgency.joblib`
- Function: `predict_editor_queue_urgency(...)`
- API field on content queue: `editor_queue_urgency`

Pitch responses use `pitch_importance` (and still write the DB column named `priority`).

## News vs article vs story pitch (do not mix)

| Thing | What it is | DB |
|-------|------------|-----|
| **News** | Event / breaking-style piece | `content` + `news` (`content_kind = news`) |
| **Article** | Feature / longer story | `content` + `article` (`content_kind = article`) |
| **Story pitch** | Idea *before* writing — meant for an **article** | `story_pitch` → later linked via `article.story_pitch_id` |

Story pitches are **not** for the `news` kind. The schema already links pitches to `article`, not to `news`.

## Flags vs topics (different things)

| Field | Where | Meaning |
|-------|-------|---------|
| `breaking_news_flag` | `news` table | Only boolean “flag” for news: is this breaking? |
| `severity` | `news` table | low / medium / high / critical (not a flag) |
| `content_kind` | `content` table | `news` or `article` |
| **topic / category** | ML + `category` table | Politics, Sports, Business, … — **not flags** |

So: **Sports / Politics / Weather are categories (topics), not flags.**  
The only news boolean flag in the schema is **`breaking_news_flag`**.

## `news_category` model — name is misleading

The folder/file is called `news_category` because the **training dataset** is the public “News Category Dataset”.

It is **not** limited to the `news` content kind. It only needs a title + text, so it can label:

- story pitches (AIA) → meant for **articles**
- article drafts (journalist suggest-category)
- news items too (same text-in → topic-out)

ML topics (trained): Politics, Sports, Business, Entertainment, World.  
Extra manual desk labels in seed: City, Weather (not required by the model).

## Other clear names

| Name | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `news_category` | Topic label for story text (name from dataset; works for articles/pitches too) |
| `engagement_score` | Rough “will people interact?” score (formula) |
| `audience_interest_score` | Rough interest intensity (formula) |
| `source_type` | `AI_GENERATED` (AIA) vs `MANUAL` |

## Layer names

| Folder | Job in one line |
|--------|-----------------|
| `api/schemas/` | JSON shapes for HTTP |
| `api/routers/` | HTTP endpoints |
| `modules/` | Thin desk façade (login user + call a service) |
| `services/` | Real business logic |
| `ml/` | Scoring / ML helpers only |
| `dao/` | SQL for one table |

==============================================================================
FILE: entities/content/README.md
==============================================================================
# Learning guide: `entities/content/`

## Why this subpackage exists

NewsMind’s data model treats **content** as the shared parent for publishable work. **News** and **article** are subtypes with extra columns; **story pitches**, **categories**, and **evidence** orbit that core. Putting all content-related dataclasses in `entities/content/` keeps imports clear: `from entities.content.content import Content`.

---

## ERD mental model

```
content (title, bodies, status, kind, source)
   ├── news (event fields, severity, breaking flag)     when content_kind = news
   ├── article (article_type, read_time)                 when content_kind = article
   └── evidence (links, citations)                       optional attachments

story_pitch — pre-publication ideas (analyst desk)
category — taxonomy for organization
```

`ContentRepository.save_content_bundle` encodes the branch: after `content.create`, create `News` or `Article` depending on `content_kind`.

Pipeline rows from CSV often arrive as **dicts** first; persistence maps them into `Content` at the last moment. Desk entry via `ContentService` builds entities explicitly.

---

## File-by-file

### `content.py` — class `Content`

Core fields: `title`, `summary`, `raw_body`, `content_body`, `content_kind`, `source_type`, `status`, `current_version`, optional `author_id`.

**`raw_body` vs `content_body`:** pipeline keeps original text in `raw_body` while cleaned/normalized text lands in `content_body`. Desk creates often set both to the same string for simplicity.

### `news.py` — class `News`

Extension row keyed by `content_id`: `event_title`, `event_type`, `event_location`, `severity`, `breaking_news_flag`, etc.

### `article.py` — class `Article`

Extension for non-breaking pieces: `article_type`, `read_time`.

### `story_pitch.py` — class `StoryPitch`

Analyst pitches: title, summary, status, priority — not yet full content.

### `category.py` — class `Category`

`category_name`, `description` for taxonomy CRUD.

### `evidence.py` — class `Evidence`

Supporting material linked to content where the ERD requires it.

### `__init__.py`

Package marker; may re-export common types for convenience.

---

## Who imports these

| Importer | Usage |
|----------|--------|
| `ContentService` | Desk creates |
| `ContentRepository` | Coordinated saves |
| `PersistenceService` | Pipeline bulk insert |
| `dao/content_dao.py` etc. | `create(entity)` type hints |

---

## Status and kind constants

Allowed `status` and `content_kind` values are **not** on the dataclass — they live in `config.constants` and are enforced by validators and `SchemaCleaner`. Entities hold whatever strings passed validation.

---

## Exercises

1. Create news via console; SELECT `content` and `news` joins in SQL — verify two rows, one id pair.
2. Ingest CSV row; follow which body field becomes `raw_body` in `PersistenceService`.
3. Add pitch; confirm no `content` row until a separate publish flow (if any) — pitches use `story_pitch` table.
4. Sketch dict → `Content` mapping in `persist_content` for one sample row.

==============================================================================
FILE: entities/README.md
==============================================================================
# Learning guide: `entities/`

## Why this folder exists

DAOs speak SQL; services speak workflows. **Entities** (dataclasses) are the typed luggage carried between those layers: a `User` object for `UserDAO.create`, an `Employee` for hire records, desk structs for profile tables.

Entities are **not** full ORM models — there is no SQLAlchemy session, no lazy loading. They are plain `@dataclass` objects with optional IDs for rows not yet inserted. This matches a learning project where you see explicit `INSERT` in DAO code.

---

## How entities connect

```
UserService.add_user  →  Employee + User entities  →  UserDAO / EmployeeDAO
ContentService.add_news  →  Content + News entities  →  ContentDAO / NewsDAO
PersistenceService  →  Content, Evidence, StoryPitch  →  ContentRepository
```

Validators run **before** entity construction in services; DAOs re-validate critical IDs and hashes at the database boundary.

Content-shaped types live in **`entities/content/`** (sub-package) because the ERD has many content-related tables — see `entities/content/README.md`.

---

## Root-level entity files

### `employee.py` — `Employee`

HR identity: `employee_code`, names, `email`, department, designation. Links 1:1 with login `users` row via `employee_id`.

### `user.py` — `User`

Login account: `employee_id`, `role_id`, `username`, `password_hash`, `account_status`. Optional display fields (`role_code`, `email`, …) exist for joined reads but are not always set on create.

### `role.py` — `Role`

Reference row for RBAC; usually seeded rather than created at runtime.

### Desk profile entities

| File | Role |
|------|------|
| `journalist.py` | Journalist desk profile |
| `editor.py` | Editor desk |
| `publisher.py` | Publisher desk |
| `audience_insight_analyst.py` | Analyst desk |
| `content_adaptation_editor.py` | Adaptation editor desk |
| `newsroom_manager.py` | Manager desk |

Each pairs with a DAO `ensure_for_user` when admin creates an account with matching `role_code`.

---

## Design conventions

- **`from __future__ import annotations`** on all modules — consistent with the rest of the repo.
- **Optional `*_id` fields** — `None` until INSERT returns id.
- **Defaults** — e.g. `User.account_status = "active"`, sensible for console demos.
- **No methods** — entities are data carriers; behavior stays in services/DAOs.

---

## ERD alignment

`tests/test_entities_erd.py` guards that entity field names still match the teaching ERD. When schema.sql changes, update entities and that test together.

---

## Exercises

1. Instantiate `User` without `user_id`, pass to `UserDAO.create`, capture returned id.
2. Compare `Employee` fields to `employee` table columns in `schema.sql`.
3. List which desk entity your demo login uses via `UserRepository.get_profile_ids`.
4. Read `entities/content/README.md` and explain content vs news vs article split.

==============================================================================
FILE: ml/adaptation/platforms.py
==============================================================================
"""Platform-specific adaptation formatting."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

from config.constants import (
    PLATFORM_EMAIL,
    PLATFORM_PRINT,
    PLATFORM_SOCIAL,
    PLATFORM_WEB,
)


def clean_title(title: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"\s+", " ", title or "").strip().rstrip(".")


def _keywords(text: str, limit: int = 2) -> list[str]:
    stop = {
        "the",
        "and",
        "for",
        "with",
        "from",
        "will",
        "this",
        "that",
        "have",
        "has",
        "was",
        "were",
        "are",
        "its",
        "into",
        "after",
        "before",
        "about",
    }
    words = re.findall(r"[A-Za-z]{4,}", (text or "").lower())
    counts = {w: words.count(w) for w in set(words) if w not in stop}
    return [w.title() for w, _ in sorted(counts.items(), key=lambda x: (-x[1], x[0]))[:limit]]


def format_output(
    platform: str,
    original_title: str,
    selected: list[tuple[int, str, float]],
) -> tuple[str, str]:
    sentences = [item[1] for item in selected]
    title = clean_title(original_title)

    if platform == PLATFORM_SOCIAL:
        content = " ".join(sentences)
        tags = " ".join("#" + k.replace(" ", "") for k in _keywords(content, 2))
        return title, f"{content} {tags}".strip()

    if platform == PLATFORM_EMAIL:
        return title, " ".join(sentences)

    if platform == PLATFORM_PRINT:
        return title, " ".join(sentences)

    # Website — paragraph chunks, no invented summaries
    chunks = []
    for i in range(0, len(sentences), 3):
        chunks.append(" ".join(sentences[i : i + 3]))
    return title, "\n\n".join(chunks)


==============================================================================
FILE: ml/adaptation/predict.py
==============================================================================
"""Select sentences for a target platform.

Uses optional joblib models under artifacts/models/<platform>/ when present;
otherwise a local heuristic (position + quotes + numbers).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

from config.constants import PLATFORM_SENTENCE_LIMITS
from ml.adaptation.sentences import split_sentences
from utils.errors import ValidationError

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
MODEL_ROOT = ROOT / "artifacts" / "models"
DEPENDENT_STARTERS = ("however", "but", "therefore", "this", "they", "it", "he", "she", "although")


def _heuristic_scores(
    sentences: list[str],
    *,
    prioritize_quotes: bool,
    prioritize_numbers: bool,
) -> list[float]:
    scored: list[float] = []
    total = max(len(sentences) - 1, 1)
    for i, sentence in enumerate(sentences):
        # Prefer lead sentences; boost quotes/numbers when requested.
        score = 0.85 - (0.55 * (i / total))
        if '"' in sentence or "“" in sentence:
            score += 0.12 if prioritize_quotes else 0.05
        if any(ch.isdigit() for ch in sentence):
            score += 0.12 if prioritize_numbers else 0.04
        if i <= 1:
            score += 0.08
        scored.append(min(1.0, max(0.05, score)))
    return scored


def _try_model_scores(title: str, sentences: list[str], platform: str) -> tuple[list[float], dict] | None:
    folder = MODEL_ROOT / platform.lower()
    model_path = folder / "model.joblib"
    meta_path = folder / "metadata.json"
    if not model_path.exists() or not meta_path.exists():
        return None
    try:
        import joblib  # optional
    except ImportError:
        return None
    try:
        model = joblib.load(model_path)
        meta = json.loads(meta_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        records = []
        for i, sentence in enumerate(sentences):
            prev = sentences[i - 1] if i > 0 else ""
            nxt = sentences[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(sentences) else ""
            records.append(
                {
                    "headline": title,
                    "sentence": sentence,
                    "context": f"PREVIOUS {prev} CURRENT {sentence} NEXT {nxt}",
                    "position": i,
                    "total_sentences": len(sentences),
                }
            )
        probs = model.predict_proba(records)[:, 1]
        return [float(p) for p in probs], meta
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — fall back to heuristic
        return None


def predict_sentences(
    title: str,
    content: str,
    platform: str,
    threshold_delta: float = 0.0,
    sentence_delta: int = 0,
    prioritize_quotes: bool = False,
    prioritize_numbers: bool = False,
) -> tuple[list[tuple[int, str, float]], dict, float]:
    sentences = split_sentences(content)
    if not sentences:
        raise ValidationError("No valid sentences found in original content.")

    model_result = _try_model_scores(title, sentences, platform)
    if model_result is not None:
        probabilities, meta = model_result
        threshold = max(0.05, min(0.95, float(meta.get("threshold", 0.5)) + threshold_delta))
        model_version = str(meta.get("version", "1.0.0"))
    else:
        probabilities = _heuristic_scores(
            sentences,
            prioritize_quotes=prioritize_quotes,
            prioritize_numbers=prioritize_numbers,
        )
        threshold = max(0.05, min(0.95, 0.45 + threshold_delta))
        meta = {"version": "heuristic-1.0.0", "threshold": threshold}
        model_version = "heuristic-1.0.0"

    scored: list[tuple[int, str, float]] = []
    for i, (sentence, prob) in enumerate(zip(sentences, probabilities)):
        boost = 0.0
        if prioritize_quotes and ('"' in sentence or "“" in sentence):
            boost += 0.15
        if prioritize_numbers and any(ch.isdigit() for ch in sentence):
            boost += 0.15
        scored.append((i, sentence, min(1.0, float(prob) + boost)))

    max_count = max(1, int(PLATFORM_SENTENCE_LIMITS.get(platform, 5)) + int(sentence_delta))
    selected = [row for row in scored if row[2] >= threshold]
    if not selected:
        selected = sorted(scored, key=lambda row: row[2], reverse=True)[:1]
    selected = sorted(selected, key=lambda row: row[2], reverse=True)[:max_count]

    indexes = {row[0] for row in selected}
    for i in list(indexes):
        if i > 0 and sentences[i].lower().startswith(DEPENDENT_STARTERS):
            indexes.add(i - 1)

    ordered = [(i, sentences[i], float(probabilities[i])) for i in sorted(indexes)]
    meta = {**meta, "version": model_version}
    return ordered, meta, threshold

FILE: ml/adaptation/README.md
==============================================================================
# CAE adaptation helpers

Used by Content Adaptation Editor APIs.

| Module | Role |
|--------|------|
| `sentences.py` | Split source text into sentences |
| `predict.py` | Select sentences per platform (heuristic; optional joblib models) |
| `platforms.py` | Format social / print / web / newsletter output |
| `validate.py` | Block invented numbers/currency vs source |

Optional models: place `artifacts/models/<platform>/model.joblib` + `metadata.json`.
Without them the API still works with the local heuristic.


==============================================================================
FILE: ml/adaptation/sentences.py
==============================================================================
"""Sentence splitting for adaptation."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

ABBREVIATION_PATTERN = re.compile(r"\b(?:Mr|Mrs|Ms|Dr|Prof|Inc|Ltd|No)\.$", re.I)
_WS = re.compile(r"\s+")


def normalize_space(text: str) -> str:
    return _WS.sub(" ", text or "").strip()


def split_sentences(text: str) -> list[str]:
    text = normalize_space(text)
    if not text:
        return []
    pieces = re.split(r'(?<=[.!?])\s+(?=[A-Z“"])', text)
    result: list[str] = []
    carry = ""
    for piece in pieces:
        piece = f"{carry} {piece}".strip() if carry else piece.strip()
        if ABBREVIATION_PATTERN.search(piece):
            carry = piece
            continue
        carry = ""
        if len(piece.split()) >= 3:
            result.append(piece)
    if carry:
        result.append(carry)
    return result


==============================================================================
FILE: ml/adaptation/validate.py
==============================================================================
"""Fact checks — block invented numbers/currency relative to source."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re


def _tokens(pattern: str, text: str) -> set[str]:
    return set(re.findall(pattern, text or "", re.I))


def validate_facts(source: str, output: str) -> dict:
    source_numbers = _tokens(r"\b\d[\d,.]*\b", source)
    output_numbers = _tokens(r"\b\d[\d,.]*\b", output)
    invented = sorted(output_numbers - source_numbers)

    source_currency = _tokens(r"(?:₹|\$|£|€)\s*\d[\d,.]*", source)
    output_currency = _tokens(r"(?:₹|\$|£|€)\s*\d[\d,.]*", output)
    invented_currency = sorted(output_currency - source_currency)

    return {
        "invented_numbers": invented,
        "invented_currency": invented_currency,
        "passed": not invented and not invented_currency,
    }

"""AIA helpers: engagement/interest formulas + category/trends."""

from ml.aia.audience_scores import (
    predict_category,
    predict_engagement,
    predict_interest,
    predict_trend,
)

__all__ = [
    "predict_engagement",
    "predict_interest",
    "predict_trend",
    "predict_category",
]


==============================================================================
FILE: ml/aia/audience_scores.py
==============================================================================
"""AIA audience scores (simple formulas + one category model).

1) Engagement score  — formula from views/likes/shares/comments
2) Audience interest — formula from how intense the interactions are
3) News category     — RandomForest joblib (topic label)
4) Trend score       — derived from category confidence

No engagement/audience joblibs — those were removed on purpose.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

from utils.logger import get_logger

log = get_logger("ml.aia.audience_scores")
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
CATEGORY_MODEL = ROOT / "artifacts" / "models" / "news_category.joblib"


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def _load_category_model() -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    if not CATEGORY_MODEL.exists():
        return None
    try:
        import joblib

        bundle = joblib.load(CATEGORY_MODEL)
        log.info("Loaded news category model from %s", CATEGORY_MODEL)
        return bundle
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
        log.warning("Could not load category model: %s", exc)
        return None


def predict_engagement(
    *,
    views: float = 120,
    likes: float = 25,
    shares: float = 8,
    comments: float = 4,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Simple engagement score from interaction counts."""
    raw = likes * 2.0 + shares * 3.0 + comments * 4.0 + views * 0.05
    score = round(min(100.0, max(0.0, raw / 10.0)), 2)
    return {
        "engagement_score": score,
        "source": "heuristic",
        "raw": round(raw, 4),
        "features_used": ["views", "likes", "shares", "comments"],
    }


def predict_interest(
    *,
    views: float = 120,
    likes: float = 25,
    shares: float = 8,
    comments: float = 4,
    engagement_raw: float | None = None,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Simple audience-interest score (interactions per view)."""
    if engagement_raw is None:
        engagement_raw = float(
            predict_engagement(views=views, likes=likes, shares=shares, comments=comments)["raw"]
        )

    intensity = (likes + shares * 2.0 + comments * 2.0) / max(float(views), 1.0)
    score = round(min(100.0, max(0.0, intensity * 100.0)), 2)

    if score >= 70:
        segment = 2  # high interest
    elif score >= 35:
        segment = 1  # medium
    else:
        segment = 0  # low

    return {
        "audience_interest_score": score,
        "source": "heuristic",
        "segment": segment,
        "distance": None,
        "engagement_raw": round(float(engagement_raw), 4),
    }


def predict_category(headline: str, body: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Predict news topic/category from headline + body."""
    bundle = _load_category_model()
    text = f"{headline or ''}. {body or ''}".strip()
    if bundle is None:
        return {
            "predicted_category": None,
            "confidence": None,
            "probabilities": {},
            "source": "fallback",
        }

    pipe = bundle["pipeline"]
    predicted = str(pipe.predict([text])[0])
    probabilities: dict[str, float] = {}
    confidence = None
    clf = pipe.named_steps.get("clf")

    # LogisticRegression / RF have predict_proba; LinearSVC uses decision_function
    if hasattr(pipe, "predict_proba"):
        values = pipe.predict_proba([text])[0]
        classes = list(clf.classes_)
        probabilities = {str(c): round(float(p), 4) for c, p in zip(classes, values)}
        confidence = round(float(max(values)), 4)
    elif clf is not None and hasattr(clf, "decision_function"):
        import numpy as np

        scores = np.asarray(clf.decision_function(pipe.named_steps["tfidf"].transform([text])))
        if scores.ndim == 1:
            # binary case
            exp = float(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-scores[0])))
            confidence = round(max(exp, 1.0 - exp), 4)
        else:
            # soft-max over decision scores for a 0..1 style confidence
            row = scores[0]
            row = row - row.max()
            exp = np.exp(row)
            probs = exp / exp.sum()
            classes = list(clf.classes_)
            probabilities = {str(c): round(float(p), 4) for c, p in zip(classes, probs)}
            confidence = round(float(probs.max()), 4)

    return {
        "predicted_category": predicted,
        "confidence": confidence,
        "probabilities": probabilities,
        "source": "model",
    }


def predict_trend(headline: str = "", body: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Trend score based on how confident the category model is."""
    category = predict_category(headline, body)
    if category.get("source") == "fallback":
        return {
            "trend_score": 65.0,
            "source": "fallback",
            "category": None,
            "category_confidence": None,
        }

    confidence = float(category.get("confidence") or 0.0)
    return {
        "trend_score": round(min(100.0, max(0.0, 35.0 + confidence * 65.0)), 2),
        "source": "model",
        "category": category.get("predicted_category"),
        "category_confidence": category.get("confidence"),
    }
